  3. Source slide page 3 (extracted text): ENGINEERING MECHANICS STATICS Equilibrium When a body is in equilibrium, the resultant of all forces acting on it is zero. Thus, the resultant force R and the resultant couple M are both zero, and we have the equilibrium equations R = ΣF = 0 M = ΣM = 0 Free-body diagram, which is a diagrammatic representation of the isolated system treated as a single body. Free-body diagram is the most important single step in the solution of problems in mechanics. S V Venkatesh - Engineering Mechanics Statics – Unit 2 – Chapter 3 - Equilibrium EV0000264 · EV0000265 · EV0000266

Transcript block 4 (source interval 000900-001199 seconds): [00:15:12.868] We have come to the end of equilibrium chapter. So basically what we have to understand in equilibrium chapter is first thing is the equations of equilibrium. For a two dimensional there are three equations of equilibrium. One is sigma [00:15:30.916] F along the vertical, algebraic sum of all the forces acting along the vertical is zero. Algebraic sum of all the forces acting along the horizontal is zero, then algebraic sum of the moment at any point should be equal to zero. So when we have these three equations, we can solve [00:15:49.988] Three unknowns. So if you have a structure which has got only three constraints or three unknowns, we can use the equations of equipment solve it. They are called as determinate structure. If you have more than three constraints, [00:16:10.180] Then the number of constraints more than three is called as a redundant reaction or constraint.
